{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01H9aaqLrMcX388dZSa4jDAe","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→# Medical Informatics Engineering — Product Research\n     2→\n     3→Researched: 2026-02-15\n     4→Developer website: https://www.webchartnow.com\n     5→\n     6→## Overview\n     7→\n     8→Medical Informatics Engineering (MIE) is a privately held health IT company founded in 1995, headquartered in Fort Wayne, Indiana. MIE was an early pioneer in cloud-based EHR systems and health information exchange — the company was \"ahead of the cloud long before 'cloud computing' became a term of art,\" deploying web-based SaaS applications for healthcare since the late 1990s. MIE was among the first 18 EHRs certified by the Certification Commission for Health Information Technology.\n     9→\n    10→Today, MIE operates three main product lines: **WebChart EHR** (ambulatory/clinical EHR), **Enterprise Health** (occupational and employee health platform, built on WebChart's technology), and **Ozwell AI** (an AI-powered health IT assistant). The company primarily serves ambulatory physician practices (from solo to multispecialty groups), occupational health providers, Fortune 500 employer health programs, hospitals and health systems, government agencies, and universities. Notable customer Concentra (a large occupational health provider) deployed MIE's platform across its worksite health centers starting around 2010.\n    11→\n    12→In April 2024, MIE received a significant growth investment from Serent Capital, a healthcare-focused PE firm with $5B AUM. The press release noted MIE had grown by an average of 40% annually over the prior six years and maintained a near-100% client retention rate. The investment was intended to expand the Enterprise Health occupational health platform. MIE markets itself as serving \"hospitals and health systems, physician practices, Fortune 500 employers, government agencies and consumers.\" Exact customer counts are not publicly disclosed.\n    13→\n    14→## Product: WebChart EHR\n    15→\n    16→CHPL IDs: 11022\n    17→\n    18→### What It Is\n    19→\n    20→WebChart EHR is a cloud-based, web-native electronic health record system for ambulatory medical practices. It is marketed as \"The minimally invasive EHR\" — emphasizing its flexibility, customizability, and ability to be adopted incrementally (module by module) rather than requiring an all-at-once implementation. The product is fully web-based — accessible from any browser without client software installation.\n    21→\n    22→WebChart is the certified product under ONC certification (CHPL ID 15.04.04.1932.WebC.84.01.0.221117, version 8.4, certified 2022-11-17). It carries a broad set of certifications: 40+ criteria spanning clinical data management (a)(1)-(a)(5), (a)(12), (a)(14)-(a)(15); transitions of care (b)(1)-(b)(3); patient portal (e)(1); clinical quality measures (c)(1)-(c)(3); public health reporting (f)(1)-(f)(2),(f)(5); FHIR APIs (g)(7),(g)(10); and various security/infrastructure criteria. This is a fully-featured ambulatory EHR certification, not a narrow single-purpose module.\n    23→\n    24→Enterprise Health, MIE's occupational health product, is built on WebChart's core platform and extends it with occupational health-specific modules. The WebChart EHR certification therefore underpins both the ambulatory EHR and the Enterprise Health product.\n    25→\n    26→### Users & Market\n    27→\n    28→**Target users**: Physicians, nurses, medical assistants, front desk staff, clinic administrators, transcriptionists, and billing staff in ambulatory settings. The CHPL SED description lists \"Medical personnel, clinic administrators.\" Enterprise Health extends users to occupational health providers, employer health program managers, and supervisors.\n    29→\n    30→**Settings**: Small to mid-sized ambulatory practices across many specialties — primary care, cardiology, orthopedic surgery, OB/GYN, dermatology, pediatrics, neurology, psychiatry, oncology, behavioral health, and particularly occupational/employee health. Also used in urgent care and worksite health centers.\n    31→\n    32→**Notable deployments**: Concentra (major occupational health provider) deployed MIE's WebChart Enterprise Health platform at worksite locations. Multiple physician testimonials cite successful paperless transitions in ambulatory settings, with cardiologists, internists, and family medicine physicians highlighted (Dr. Stephen Beyer, Dr. Jay Alexander, Dr. John Dickins, Dr. Michael Mirro — per HealthITOutcomes article).\n    33→\n    34→**Pricing**: Starts at $350/month per provider, with costs varying based on clinician count and selected modules (per FindEMR).\n    35→\n    36→### Modules & Functionality\n    37→\n    38→WebChart EHR is a comprehensive ambulatory EHR with extensive modular functionality. results\n   209→\n   210→### Data & Content\n   211→\n   212→Based on the features documented above, WebChart EHR stores and manages a very broad range of clinical and administrative data:\n   213→\n   214→**Clinical records**: Patient demographics, medical history (medical, surgical, family, social, gynecological, pregnancy), encounters/visit notes, chief complaints, review of systems, physical examination findings, vitals, diagnoses (ICD-11), clinical assessments, and depart instructions.\n   215→\n   216→**Medication data**: Prescriptions (including controlled substances), medication lists, allergy/intolerance records, drug interaction data, refill requests, SureScripts medication history, formulary information.\n   217→\n   218→**Orders & results**: Lab orders and results, observation data with flowsheets, vital sign tracking, imaging orders.\n   219→\n   220→**Documents**: Scanned paper documents (TIFF), PDFs, Word documents, photos/pictures, sketches, forms, faxes (inbound and outbound), DICOM images, dictation audio files, transcriptions.\n   221→\n   222→**Immunizations**: Injection records, vial tracking, vaccine compliance data, immunization registry submissions.\n   223→\n   224→**Scheduling**: Appointments, waiting lists, check-in records, cancellations, no-shows.\n   225→\n   226→**Portal data**: Patient portal messages, health questionnaires, portal activation/access records, self-check-in data.\n   227→\n   228→**Occupational health data**: Case records (OSHA, injury/illness), work restrictions and accommodations, work status, exposure records, health surveillance panel memberships and results, respirator fit data, audiometric data, PFT results, vision test results, biometric data, EKG data, X-ray results, DOT fitness determinations, medical clearances, absence records, industrial hygiene data.\n   229→\n   230→**Financial/billing data**: Insurance information and eligibility, deals, fee schedules, EOBs, pending billing records. The marketing site describes \"Revenue Cycle Management\" with billing and claims processing, though the detailed documentation also mentions OpenPM integration, suggesting billing may be partially handled by an external practice management system (OpenPM) in some deployments.\n   231→\n   232→**Quality/compliance data**: Quality measure data, PI reporting data, OSHA 300/300A/301 case data, RAF scores.\n   233→\n   234→**Administrative data**: User accounts, security roles, audit logs, task records, e-signature records, system configuration, interface/integration configuration, asset management records (equipment, calibration, maintenance).\n   235→\n   236→**Key uncertainty**: While WebChart clearly has billing-adjacent features (insurance eligibility, fee schedules, deals, pending billing reports, EOB scanning, revenue cycle management), it's somewhat unclear whether full claims submission and billing is entirely built-in or whether some deployments rely on separate practice management/billing systems (e.g., OpenPM) for the complete billing workflow. The marketing site describes integrated RCM, but the documentation's references to \"OpenPM export\" suggest a hybrid approach may be common.\n   237→\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. MIE was an early pioneer in cloud-based EHR systems and health information exchange — the company was \"ahead of the cloud long before 'cloud computing' became a term of art,\" deploying web-based SaaS applications for healthcare since the late 1990s. MIE was among the first 18 EHRs certified by the Certification Commission for Health Information Technology.\n\nToday, MIE operates three main product lines: **WebChart EHR** (ambulatory/clinical EHR), **Enterprise Health** (occupational and employee health platform, built on WebChart's technology), and **Ozwell AI** (an AI-powered health IT assistant). The company primarily serves ambulatory physician practices (from solo to multispecialty groups), occupational health providers, Fortune 500 employer health programs, hospitals and health systems, government agencies, and universities. Notable customer Concentra (a large occupational health provider) deployed MIE's platform across its worksite health centers starting around 2010.\n\nIn April 2024, MIE received a significant growth investment from Serent Capital, a healthcare-focused PE firm with $5B AUM. The press release noted MIE had grown by an average of 40% annually over the prior six years and maintained a near-100% client retention rate. The investment was intended to expand the Enterprise Health occupational health platform. MIE markets itself as serving \"hospitals and health systems, physician practices, Fortune 500 employers, government agencies and consumers.\" Exact customer counts are not publicly disclosed.\n\n## Product: WebChart EHR\n\nCHPL IDs: 11022\n\n### What It Is\n\nWebChart EHR is a cloud-based, web-native electronic health record system for ambulatory medical practices. It is marketed as \"The minimally invasive EHR\" — emphasizing its flexibility, customizability, and ability to be adopted incrementally (module by module) rather than requiring an all-at-once implementation. The product is fully web-based — accessible from any browser without client software installation.\n\nWebChart is the certified product under ONC certification (CHPL ID 15.04.04.1932.WebC.84.01.0.221117, version 8.4, certified 2022-11-17). It carries a broad set of certifications: 40+ criteria spanning clinical data management (a)(1)-(a)(5), (a)(12), (a)(14)-(a)(15); transitions of care (b)(1)-(b)(3); patient portal (e)(1); clinical quality measures (c)(1)-(c)(3); public health reporting (f)(1)-(f)(2),(f)(5); FHIR APIs (g)(7),(g)(10); and various security/infrastructure criteria. This is a fully-featured ambulatory EHR certification, not a narrow single-purpose module.\n\nEnterprise Health, MIE's occupational health product, is built on WebChart's core platform and extends it with occupational health-specific modules. The WebChart EHR certification therefore underpins both the ambulatory EHR and the Enterprise Health product.\n\n### Users & Market\n\n**Target users**: Physicians, nurses, medical assistants, front desk staff, clinic administrators, transcriptionists, and billing staff in ambulatory settings. The CHPL SED description lists \"Medical personnel, clinic administrators.\" Enterprise Health extends users to occupational health providers, employer health program managers, and supervisors.\n\n**Settings**: Small to mid-sized ambulatory practices across many specialties — primary care, cardiology, orthopedic surgery, OB/GYN, dermatology, pediatrics, neurology, psychiatry, oncology, behavioral health, and particularly occupational/employee health. Also used in urgent care and worksite health centers.\n\n**Notable deployments**: Concentra (major occupational health provider) deployed MIE's WebChart Enterprise Health platform at worksite locations. Multiple physician testimonials cite successful paperless transitions in ambulatory settings, with cardiologists, internists, and family medicine physicians highlighted (Dr. Stephen Beyer, Dr. Jay Alexander, Dr. John Dickins, Dr. Michael Mirro — per HealthITOutcomes article).\n\n**Pricing**: Starts at $350/month per provider, with costs varying based on clinician count and selected modules (per FindEMR).\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nWebChart EHR is a comprehensive ambulatory EHR with extensive modular functionality. Based on the vendor's documentation site (docs.webchartnow.com) and marketing materials, the product covers:\n\n**Clinical Documentation & Encounters**:\n- Customizable encounter templates by specialty with point-and-click exams\n- Chief complaint, HPI, past medical/surgical/family/social history\n- Review of systems, physical examination, vitals\n- Symptoms/diagnosis management with ICD-11 support\n- E&M coding calculator\n- Encounter protocols and workflows\n- Visit and legacy encounter types\n- Clinical education and depart instructions\n- Carbon copy functionality for encounter sharing\n\n**Medication Management & E-Prescribing**:\n- Full e-prescribing including EPCS (Electronic Prescribing for Controlled Substances) with IdenTrust certificate support\n- SureScripts integration for prescription routing, medication history reconciliation, formulary/dosing information\n- Drug interaction/allergy warnings and clinical decision support\n- E-refill management (pending, errors, all refills)\n- Drug guide search and MedicalCodify integration\n- Drug plan and formulary lookups\n- Remembered prescriptions and medication libraries\n- Travel kits\n\n**Order & Result Management**:\n- Computerized provider order entry (CPOE)\n- Lab orders with real-time lab interfaces\n- Order tracking with requisitions\n- Observation flowsheets and vitals tracking\n- Results review workflows\n\n**Document Management**:\n- Full document management system supporting scanned TIFFs, text, Word, PDF, pictures/photos, sketches, and forms\n- Web-based and high-speed scanning with barcode indexing\n- Bubble form support for standardized data capture\n- Inbound fax queue management and outbound faxing\n- Print definitions for documents, forms, and chart printing\n- DICOM imaging: viewer, modality worklist (MWL), CD/DVD burning\n- RadOmni and TechOmni interfaces for radiology workflow\n- MammoTrack for mammography tracking\n\n**Scheduling**:\n- Appointment scheduling with multiple views\n- Patient/appointment wizard\n- Waiting lists\n- Check-in functionality\n- Guided template schedules\n- Cancellation/no-show tracking\n- Email appointment reminders\n\n**Patient Portal & Engagement**:\n- Patient portal with secure access to health information\n- Self check-in capability\n- Health questionnaires\n- Portal activation codes\n- Secure messaging from portal\n- Virtual waiting room\n- NoMoreClipboard integration\n- Applicant portal (for employee health use cases)\n- Employer/employee portal\n- Supervisor portal with scheduling and questionnaire capabilities\n\n**Injection & Immunization Management**:\n- Injection tracking with vial management\n- CHIRP (state immunization registry) interface\n- Mass injection recording\n- Vaccine compliance tracking\n\n**Case Management & Injury Care** (particularly strong for occupational health):\n- Case types: OSHA, hospital, absence management, MSEA\n- Restriction tracking and editing (work restrictions/accommodations)\n- Work status tracking\n- Multiple exposure management\n- Lost time tracking\n\n**Health Surveillance & Compliance** (occupational health focus):\n- Health surveillance panels with automated and manual membership\n- Panel action evaluator and action rules\n- Due list management\n- Respirator information tracking\n- Industrial hygiene integration\n- Medical clearance workflows\n- DOT fitness determination exams\n\n**Occupational Health Programs**:\n- Occupational medicine workflows\n- Worksite injury & illness tracking\n- Absence management\n- Health risk assessment\n- Employee assistance program\n- Medical surveillance programs\n\n**Dictation & Transcription**:\n- MIEPlayer for voice recording\n- SpeechMike dictation support\n- Dictation routing and editing\n- Transcription workflows\n- Whisper AI dictation and summarization (via Ozwell AI)\n\n**Quality & Reporting**:\n- Quality reporting enrollment and provider status tracking\n- Promoting Interoperability (PI) measures\n- OSHA 300 Log, 300A, and 300/301 reporting\n- 50+ specialized reports (activity logs, appointments, cases, demographics, encounters, medications, observations)\n- DataVis grid data tools\n- CSV export\n- RAF Score (Risk Adjustment Factor)\n- OpenPM export capabilities\n\n**Financial/Billing**:\n- Deal management and financial encounter sections\n- Pending billing reports\n- Insurance eligibility checking\n- Insurance summary management\n- Fee schedules\n- EOB (Explanation of Benefits) scanning\n- Integration with practice management and billing systems\n- Revenue cycle management described on marketing site\n\n**E-Signature**:\n- Electronic signature workflows\n- Request and un-request e-signature capabilities\n- Pending review workflows\n\n**Task Management**:\n- Task lists with search\n- Fast task templates\n- Delegated and pending task tracking\n\n**Integrations & Interoperability**:\n- Over 300 vendor integrations claimed\n- HL7 interfaces\n- DICOM\n- IHE profiles\n- CCR/CDA document support\n- FHIR APIs (g)(7) and (g)(10)\n- Lab interfaces (real-time)\n- Pharmacy interfaces (SureScripts)\n- Immunization registry interfaces (CHIRP)\n- Custom pharmacy setup\n- Interface manager for managing connections\n- Datasend queue for outbound data\n- ODBC connectivity\n- CSV import/export APIs for clinical encounters, injections, observations, PFT data, vital signs, lab results\n\n**System Administration**:\n- User/provider and department management\n- Security roles with comparison tools\n- SSO (SAML, ADFS)\n- Multi-factor authentication\n- Audit logging\n- Chart merging\n- Partition management\n- Multilingual support\n- Translation manager\n- Asset management module (equipment tracking, warranties, maintenance, calibration)\n- Scheduled jobs\n- Auto-routing rules\n\n**Data Migration**:\n- ETL tools and standardized import formats\n- Specialized importers for employees, health surveillance, audiometric data, PFT, vital signs, cases, panel membership\n- CCR document import\n- Chart file export\n\n**Specialty Data Entry**:\n- Audiogram data entry\n- Vision testing\n- Biometric data\n- EKG data\n- PFT (pulmonary function testing)\n- X-ray results\n\n### Data & Content\n\nBased on the features documented above, WebChart EHR stores and manages a very broad range of clinical and administrative data:\n\n**Clinical records**: Patient demographics, medical history (medical, surgical, family, social, gynecological, pregnancy), encounters/visit notes, chief complaints, review of systems, physical examination findings, vitals, diagnoses (ICD-11), clinical assessments, and depart instructions.\n\n**Medication data**: Prescriptions (including controlled substances), medication lists, allergy/intolerance records, drug interaction data, refill requests, SureScripts medication history, formulary information.\n\n**Orders & results**: Lab orders and results, observation data with flowsheets, vital sign tracking, imaging orders.\n\n**Documents**: Scanned paper documents (TIFF), PDFs, Word documents, photos/pictures, sketches, forms, faxes (inbound and outbound), DICOM images, dictation audio files, transcriptions.\n\n**Immunizations**: Injection records, vial tracking, vaccine compliance data, immunization registry submissions.\n\n**Scheduling**: Appointments, waiting lists, check-in records, cancellations, no-shows.\n\n**Portal data**: Patient portal messages, health questionnaires, portal activation/access records, self-check-in data.\n\n**Occupational health data**: Case records (OSHA, injury/illness), work restrictions and accommodations, work status, exposure records, health surveillance panel memberships and results, respirator fit data, audiometric data, PFT results, vision test results, biometric data, EKG data, X-ray results, DOT fitness determinations, medical clearances, absence records, industrial hygiene data.\n\n**Financial/billing data**: Insurance information and eligibility, deals, fee schedules, EOBs, pending billing records. The marketing site describes \"Revenue Cycle Management\" with billing and claims processing, though the detailed documentation also mentions OpenPM integration, suggesting billing may be partially handled by an external practice management system (OpenPM) in some deployments.\n\n**Quality/compliance data**: Quality measure data, PI reporting data, OSHA 300/300A/301 case data, RAF scores.\n\n**Administrative data**: User accounts, security roles, audit logs, task records, e-signature records, system configuration, interface/integration configuration, asset management records (equipment, calibration, maintenance).\n\n**Key uncertainty**: While WebChart clearly has billing-adjacent features (insurance eligibility, fee schedules, deals, pending billing reports, EOB scanning, revenue cycle management), it's somewhat unclear whether full claims submission and billing is entirely built-in or whether some deployments rely on separate practice management/billing systems (e.g., OpenPM) for the complete billing workflow. The marketing site describes integrated RCM, but the documentation's references to \"OpenPM export\" suggest a hybrid approach may be common.\n","numLines":237,"startLine":1,"totalLines":237}}}

[#introduction] \nThe Electronic Health Information (EHI) Export provides authorized users with the ability to download the designated record set (DRS) as defined by To support this process, the system performing the export should maintain the following minimum requirements: \n8GB RAM \nAvailable hard drive space of at least 100MB/patient being exported \nIn the event a customer does not have the hardware to perform such a process, assistance from MIE can be requested. \nDesignated Record Set Content  [#designated-record-set-content] \nObject \nDescription To support this process, the system performing the export should maintain the following minimum requirements:\n\n* 8GB RAM\n* Available hard drive space of at least 100MB/patient being exported\n\nIn the event a customer does not have the hardware to perform such a process, assistance from MIE can be requested.\n\n## Designated Record Set Content\n\n<table>\n<tr>\n<td><strong>Object</strong></td>\n<td><strong>Description</strong></td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>accommodation_types</td>\n<td>Short codes for accommodations paired with a longer description</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>accommodations</td>\n<td>Modifications that allow an employee to continue working while not permitted to perform some aspect of their job</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>appointments</td>\n<td>Patient appointment details</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>apt_types</td>\n<td>Coded reasons for a patient appointment</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>audio</td>\n<td>Discrete data for occupational audiogram tests</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>audio_baseline</td>\n<td>Baseline audiogram tests for comparison with future results</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>body_part_types</td>\n<td>Descriptions for location of occupational injury</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>clinical_restriction_types</td>\n<td>Short codes for clinical restrictions paired with a longer description</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>coding_link</td>\n<td>Generic linking information between patient chart content and a code system/code pair</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>conversation_messages</td>\n<td>Messages between a patient or representative and their care team</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td>dicom_images</td>\n<td>DICOM image files provided in Base64</td>\n</tr>","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false}}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_01JnsMugzUFWjHPvQPmvhgMj","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01XD8V3US6bggHxVB2D2Homt","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/home/jmandel/hobby/ehi-export-analysis/results/medical-informatics-engineering--webchart-ehr/downloads/enrichment/extract-schema.ts","content":"#!/usr/bin/env bun\n/**\n * Extracts a queryable schema from the WebChart EHI Export documentation.\n *\n * Inputs:\n *   - ../ehi-export-technical-details.md  (markdown source with DRS table + sample JSON)\n *   - ../sample-single-patient-export.json (the sample export JSON)\n *\n * Outputs:\n *   - drs-data-dictionary.json   — object names + descriptions from the DRS table\n *   - export-schema.json         — field-level schema inferred from the sample export\n *   - extraction-report.json     — coverage/accounting output\n */\n\nimport { readFileSync, writeFileSync } from \"fs\";\nimport { join, dirname } from \"path\";\n\nconst BASE = dirname(import.meta.path);\nconst DOWNLOADS = join(BASE, \"..\");\n\n// ─── 1. Parse the DRS data dictionary table from markdown ───\n\nconst md = readFileSync(join(DOWNLOADS, \"ehi-export-technical-details.md\"), \"utf-8\");\n\ninterface DRSObject {\n  object_name: string;\n  description: string;\n}\n\nfunction parseDRSTable(markdown: string): DRSObject[] {\n  const objects: DRSObject[] = [];\n\n  // The DRS table is in HTML format: <tr><td>name</td><td>description</td></tr>\n  const tableStart = markdown.indexOf(\"## Designated Record Set Content\");\n  const tableEnd = markdown.indexOf(\"## Example Export Files\");\n  if (tableStart === -1 || tableEnd === -1) {\n    throw new Error(\"Could not find DRS table boundaries\");\n  }\n\n  const tableSection = markdown.slice(tableStart, tableEnd);\n\n  // Parse <tr><td>...</td><td>...</td></tr> pairs\n  const rowRegex = /<tr>\\s*<td>(.*?)<\\/td>\\s*<td>(.*?)<\\/td>\\s*<\\/tr>/gs;\n  let match;\n  while ((match = rowRegex.exec(tableSection)) !== null) {\n    const name = match[1].replace(/<[^>]*>/g, \"\").trim();\n    const desc = match[2].replace(/<[^>]*>/g, \"\").trim();\n    // Skip the header row\n    if (name === \"**Object**\" || name === \"Object\") continue;\n    objects.push({ object_name: name, description: desc });\n  }\n\n  return objects;\n}\n\nconst drsObjects = parseDRSTable(md);\nconsole.log(`Parsed ${drsObjects.length} DRS objects from markdown table`);\n\nwriteFileSync(\n  join(BASE, \"drs-data-dictionary.json\"),\n  JSON.stringify(drsObjects, null, 2)\n);\n\n// ─── 2. Infer field-level schema from sample export JSON ───\n\nconst sampleExport = JSON.parse(\n  readFileSync(join(DOWNLOADS, \"sample-single-patient-export.json\"), \"utf-8\")\n);\n\ninterface FieldSchema {\n  name: string;\n  type: \"string\" | \"number\" | \"boolean\" | \"null\" | \"object\" | \"array\" | \"mixed\";\n  sample_value?: string;\n  /** For objects: nested field schemas */\n  fields?: FieldSchema[];\n  /** For arrays: schema of array items */\n  item_schema?: FieldSchema[];\n  /** Number of items in sample (for arrays) */\n  sample_count?: number;\n}\n\ninterface ObjectSchema {\n  object_name: string;\n  description: string;\n  relationship_key: string; // e.g. \"pat_id\" for \"accommodations.pat_id\"\n  fields: FieldSchema[];\n  sample_record_count: number;\n}\n\ninterface ExportSchema {\n  format: string;\n  top_level_keys: string[];\n  request_pattern: string;\n  patient_fields: FieldSchema[];\n  nested_objects: ObjectSchema[];\n}\n\nfunction inferType(value: unknown): FieldSchema[\"type\"] {\n  if (value === null) return \"null\";\n  if (Array.isArray(value)) return \"array\";\n  return typeof value as FieldSchema[\"type\"];\n}\n\nfunction truncateSample(value: unknown): string {\n  const s = JSON.stringify(value);\n  if (s.length > 100) return s.slice(0, 97) + \"...\";\n  return s;\n}\n\nfunction inferFieldSchema(key: string, value: unknown, maxDepth = 2): FieldSchema {\n  const type = inferType(value);\n  const schema: FieldSchema = { name: key, type };\n\n  if (type === \"string\" || type === \"number\" || type === \"boolean\") {\n    schema.sample_value = truncateSample(value);\n  } else if (type === \"null\") {\n    schema.sample_value = \"null\";\n  } else if (type === \"object\" && value !== null && maxDepth > 0) {\n    schema.fields = Object.entries(value as Record<string, unknown>).map(\n      ([k, v]) => inferFieldSchema(k, v, maxDepth - 1)\n    );\n  } else if (type === \"array\" && maxDepth > 0) {\n    const arr = value as unknown[];\n    schema.sample_count = arr.length;\n    if (arr.length > 0 && typeof arr[0] === \"object\" && arr[0] !== null) {\n      schema.item_schema = Object.entries(arr[0] as Record<string, unknown>).map(\n        ([k, v]) => inferFieldSchema(k, v, maxDepth - Downloaded the raw markdown source files, which are cleaner than the rendered HTML and include YAML frontmatter metadata:\n\n```bash\ncurl -sL \"https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mieweb/docs/master/content/resources/system-specifications/electronic-health-information-ehi-export/ehi-export-technical-details.md\" -o ehi-export-technical-details.md\ncurl -sL \"https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mieweb/docs/master/content/resources/system-specifications/electronic-health-information-ehi-export/ehi-export-for-end-users.md\" -o ehi-export-for-end-users.md\n```\n\n7. **Extracted the sample JSON export** embedded in the technical details page — a complete single-patient export (482KB) with actual field-level data for patient \"William S. Hart\".\n\n8. **Checked Google Drive source documents** (linked via \"Edit in Google Drive\" buttons). Both require Google authentication — not publicly accessible. The published HTML/markdown versions contain the complete content.\n\n9. **Built enrichment scripts** to extract queryable JSON from the documentation: a data dictionary (99 DRS objects) and a field-level schema inferred from the sample export.\n\n## What Was Found\n\n### Export Format\n\nThe WebChart EHI export produces data in **JSON format** — a custom, proprietary JSON structure that maps directly to the product's internal MySQL database schema. This is a genuine (b)(10) export, not a FHIR wrapper.\n\nKey structural elements:\n- **Top-level envelope**: `{ \"request\", \"documentation\", \"db\", \"meta\" }`\n- **Request pattern**: `db/patients/{pat_id}/drs` — retrieves the Designated Record Set for a patient\n- **Patient data**: The `db` array contains one object per patient, with:\n  - 61+ scalar fields for demographics, contact info, identifiers (SSN, MRN), insurance, and dates\n  - 33+ nested arrays keyed as `{table_name}.{foreign_key}` (e.g., `accommodations.pat_id`, `documents.pat_id`, `encounters.pat_id`)\n  - Foreign key references to `users` and other lookup tables are resolved inline (the full user record is embedded wherever a user_id reference occurs)\n- **Multi-patient exports**: Generate one JSON file per patient, then compress all into a ZIP archive\n- **Binary content**: Documents and DICOM images are provided as Base64-encoded strings within the JSON\n\n### Data Dictionary (Designated Record Set Content)\n\nThe documentation enumerates **99 database objects** included in the DRS export:\n\n| Category | Objects | Examples |\n|----------|---------|----------|\n| Patient demographics | 7 | patients, patient_mrns, patient_extended_values, patient_additional_info, patient_partitions, pat_pat_relations, pat_pat_relflat |\n| Clinical encounters | 8 | encounters, encounter_orders, encounter_documents, encounter_dictation, encounter_extended_values, encounter_extended_index, encounter_order_status, encounters_link |\n| Medications & prescriptions | 12 | rxlist, rxlist_allergylist, rxlist_refills, rxlist_sign, escript_newrx, escript_refreq, escript_refres_sign, escript_cancelrx, escript_changereq, escript_error, escript_message, escript_rxfill |\n| Documents & imaging | 10 | documents, documents_txt, documents_link, document_attachments, document_sign, document_types, dicom_studies, dicom_series, dicom_images, dictation |\n| Orders & labs | 10 | order_requests, order_items, order_list, order_questions, order_question_answers, order_request_icd9cm, lab_requests, lab_requests_txt, observations, observation_codes |\n| Conditions & procedures | 4 | patient_conditions, patient_conditions_family, patient_procedures, coding_link |\n| Occupational health | 15 | incidents, incident_bls, incident_nibp, incident_links, incident_condition_link, incident_extended_fields, incident_users, accommodations, patient_clinical_restriction, audio, audio_baseline, pft, pft_maneuver, panel, panel_membership |\n| Insurance & billing | 4 | insurance_policy, insurance_plans, insurance_pre_cert |\n| Appointments | 4 | appointments, apt_types, multi_referrer_apt, multi_resource_apt, multi_type_apt |\n| Tasks & messaging | 4 | tasks, tasklist_extended_values, conversation_messages, extended_value_names |\n| Injections/immunizations | 1 | injections |\n| Reference/lookup tables | 10+ | accommodation_types, body_part_types, clinical_restriction_types, encounter_visit_types, document_types, nature_of_injury_types, observation_codes, storage_types, relation_types, users, locations |\n\nOf these 99 objects, 81 have descriptions; 18 (mostly escript_* and incident_* tables) have names only.\n\n### Export Interface\n\nThe export is initiated from **Control Panel → Interfaces → EHI Export** within WebChart:\n- **Administrators/SuperUsers** can export: a single patient, all patients for a provider, or all patients in the system\n- **Clinical users** can export: a single patient only\n- System requirements: 8GB RAM, ~100MB disk per patient exported\n- MIE offers assistance for customers lacking hardware capacity\n\n### Sample Export\n\nThe documentation includes a complete single-patient sample export (482KB JSON) for a test patient \"William S. Hart\" (pat_id 18, DOB 1954-11-30). The sample demonstrates:\n- Full demographic fields including SSN, insurance, emergency contacts\n- 2 appointments, 9 documents, 2 encounter orders, 1 encounter\n- 1 incident (workplace injury), 2 insurance policies\n- 3 patient conditions, 2 family conditions, 1 procedure\n- 2 medications, 2 allergies, 1 accommodation, 1 clinical restriction\n- Documents with Base64-encoded content\n- User references resolved inline with full user records\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nThis is one of the more thorough (b)(10) implementations encountered. The export explicitly addresses the **designated record set** by name and maps to internal database tables rather than to a standardized clinical model. This means it naturally captures data that a FHIR-only approach would miss.\n\n**Clearly covered domains:**\n- Demographics and identifiers (patients, patient_mrns, patient_extended_values)\n- Clinical encounters with full encounter data (encounters + extended values + orders + documents)\n- Medications and prescriptions, including full e-prescribing history (rxlist, escript_* tables)\n- Allergies (rxlist_allergylist)\n- Conditions, diagnoses, and problems (patient_conditions, patient_conditions_family)\n- Procedures (patient_procedures)\n- Lab results and observations (observations, lab_requests, observation_codes)\n- Orders (order_requests, order_items)\n- Documents with binary content in Base64 (documents, documents_txt, document_attachments)\n- DICOM imaging data (dicom_studies, dicom_series, dicom_images)\n- Insurance policies (insurance_policy, insurance_plans, insurance_pre_cert)\n- Appointments (appointments, apt_types)\n- Injections/immunizations (injections)\n- Dictation and transcription (dictation, dictation_documents, encounter_dictation)\n- Patient-provider messaging (conversation_messages)\n- Tasks and care coordination (tasks, tasklist_extended_values)\n- E-signatures (document_sign)\n- Patient relationships (pat_pat_relations, user_patients)\n\n**Strong occupational health coverage:**\n- Workplace incidents with full detail (incidents, incident_bls, incident_nibp, incident_links, incident_condition_link, incident_extended_fields)\n- Work accommodations and restrictions (accommodations, patient_clinical_restriction, accommodation_types, clinical_restriction_types)\n- Health surveillance panels (panel, panel_membership, panel_decert, patient_panel_status)\n- Audiometric data (audio, audio_baseline)\n- Pulmonary function tests (pft, pft_maneuver)\n- Respirator fit data (patient_respiratordetails)\n\n**Potentially missing or unclear:**\n- **Billing/claims detail**: While `insurance_policy` and `insurance_pre_cert` cover insurance and prior authorizations, there are no tables for charges, claims, payments, EOBs, or fee schedules. Given that WebChart integrates with external billing systems (OpenPM), detailed billing records may live outside the EHR. This is likely a genuine boundary of what WebChart stores rather than an export omission.\n- **Vitals as a distinct entity**: Vital signs appear to be stored in the generic `observations` table rather than as a separate entity, which is appropriate.\n- **Care plans and goals**: Not present as named entities, though they may be captured as encounter data or documents.\n- **Referrals**: No explicit referral table, though referral information may be captured in orders or documents.\n- **Patient portal data beyond messaging**: Portal questionnaire responses, self-check-in data — not clearly represented. May be captured as documents or observations.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\n- **Format**: Custom JSON mapping directly to MySQL database tables\n- **Standard**: Proprietary — not FHIR, C-CDA, or any external standard\n- **Appropriateness**: Highly appropriate for the data. A database-native export captures the full breadth of the product's data model, including occupational health data, incident details, and extended values that would be impossible to represent in FHIR US Core. This is exactly what (b)(10) should look like.\n- **Relationships**: Foreign keys are expressed through the nested array naming convention (`table.foreign_key`) and through inline resolution of referenced records (e.g., user records are embedded at every user_id reference). This is verbose but self-contained.\n- **Binary data**: Documents and images are Base64-encoded inline in the JSON. For large patient records with many documents, this could produce very large files (the docs suggest ~100MB per patient).\n- **Reconstructability**: A third party could reconstruct the patient record with reasonable effort. The JSON structure is self-documenting through field names, and the data dictionary provides table-level descriptions. However, **field-level descriptions are absent** — you get field names and sample values but must infer meaning from names alone.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n**Strengths:**\n- Clear, well-structured documentation on a modern documentation site\n- Explicit reference to 45 CFR 164.501 and §170.315(b)(10) — the vendor understands the regulatory requirement\n- Complete sample export with realistic data for a test patient\n- 99 database objects enumerated with descriptions\n- Export instructions with screenshots for both admin and clinical users\n- System requirements documented\n- Multi-patient export behavior documented (ZIP of individual JSON files)\n\n**Weaknesses:**\n- **No field-level documentation**: The data dictionary lists 99 objects with one-line descriptions, but there is no documentation of individual fields within those objects. A developer must infer field meanings from names and sample values.\n- **18 objects have no descriptions at all** — just names (e.g., escript_cancelrx, incident_bls, observation_flags)\n- **No data types specified**: All values in the JSON are strings (even numeric values like IDs), so there's no schema for intended types\n- **No value sets documented**: Coded fields (status codes, type codes) are not documented with their possible values\n- **No relationship/ERD documentation**: The foreign key relationships between tables are implied by the naming convention but not formally documented\n- **Single sample only**: Only one test patient is shown; no examples of complex multi-encounter, multi-document patients\n- **Date format**: Dates use MySQL's `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS` format; sentinel values like `0000-00-00 00:00:00` appear without explanation\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n- **Granularity**: Table-level names and descriptions ✓; Field-level names ✓ (in sample); Field-level descriptions ✗; Data types ✗; Value sets ✗; Cardinality ✗\n- **Coded field documentation**: Not present. Fields like `status`, `confirmed`, `canceled` use numeric codes without documented meanings.\n- **Relationship documentation**: Implicit through naming convention only. No entity-relationship diagram.\n- **Versioning**: The markdown frontmatter shows `date: 2023-11-16`, consistent with the published date. No change history.\n\nOverall, the data dictionary is **table-level only**, not field-level. The sample export fills the gap somewhat by showing actual field names and values, but a developer implementing an import would need significant reverse-engineering effort for coded fields, status values, and date conventions.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL (after redirects): https://docs.webchartnow.com/resources/system-specifications/electronic-health-information-ehi-export/ehi-export-technical-details/\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no (static HTML, no JavaScript required for content)\n- Navigation complexity: direct_link (registered URL contains all technical documentation)\n- Anti-bot issues: none (Cloudflare CDN but no challenge)\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n\n- **Google Drive source documents** (linked via \"Edit in Google Drive\" buttons on both pages) require Google authentication and are not publicly accessible. The published HTML/markdown versions contain the complete content, so this is not a barrier.\n- **No downloadable files**: The documentation is entirely web-based — no PDFs, ZIPs, CSVs, or schema files are available for download. The published HTML/markdown versions contain the complete content.\n\n9. **Built enrichment scripts** to extract queryable JSON from the documentation: a data dictionary (99 DRS objects) and a field-level schema inferred from the sample export.\n\n## What Was Found\n\n### Export Format\n\nThe WebChart EHI export produces data in **JSON format** — a custom, proprietary JSON structure that maps directly to the product's internal MySQL database schema. This is a genuine (b)(10) export, not a FHIR wrapper.\n\nKey structural elements:\n- **Top-level envelope**: `{ \"request\", \"documentation\", \"db\", \"meta\" }`\n- **Request pattern**: `db/patients/{pat_id}/drs` — retrieves the Designated Record Set for a patient\n- **Patient data**: The `db` array contains one object per patient, with:\n  - 61+ scalar fields for demographics, contact info, identifiers (SSN, MRN), insurance, and dates\n  - 33+ nested arrays keyed as `{table_name}.{foreign_key}` (e.g., `accommodations.pat_id`, `documents.pat_id`, `encounters.pat_id`)\n  - Foreign key references to `users` and other lookup tables are resolved inline (the full user record is embedded wherever a user_id reference occurs)\n- **Multi-patient exports**: Generate one JSON file per patient, then compress all into a ZIP archive\n- **Binary content**: Documents and DICOM images are provided as Base64-encoded strings within the JSON\n\n### Data Dictionary (Designated Record Set Content)\n\nThe documentation enumerates **99 database objects** included in the DRS export:\n\n| Category | Objects | Examples |\n|----------|---------|----------|\n| Patient demographics | 7 | patients, patient_mrns, patient_extended_values, patient_additional_info, patient_partitions, pat_pat_relations, pat_pat_relflat |\n| Clinical encounters | 8 | encounters, encounter_orders, encounter_documents, encounter_dictation, encounter_extended_values, encounter_extended_index, encounter_order_status, encounters_link |\n| Medications & prescriptions | 12 | rxlist, rxlist_allergylist, rxlist_refills, rxlist_sign, escript_newrx, escript_refreq, escript_refres_sign, escript_cancelrx, escript_changereq, escript_error, escript_message, escript_rxfill |\n| Documents & imaging | 10 | documents, documents_txt, documents_link, document_attachments, document_sign, document_types, dicom_studies, dicom_series, dicom_images, dictation |\n| Orders & labs | 10 | order_requests, order_items, order_list, order_questions, order_question_answers, order_request_icd9cm, lab_requests, lab_requests_txt, observations, observation_codes |\n| Conditions & procedures | 4 | patient_conditions, patient_conditions_family, patient_procedures, coding_link |\n| Occupational health | 15 | incidents, incident_bls, incident_nibp, incident_links, incident_condition_link, incident_extended_fields, incident_users, accommodations, patient_clinical_restriction, audio, audio_baseline, pft, pft_maneuver, panel, panel_membership |\n| Insurance & billing | 4 | insurance_policy, insurance_plans, insurance_pre_cert |\n| Appointments | 4 | appointments, apt_types, multi_referrer_apt, multi_resource_apt, multi_type_apt |\n| Tasks & messaging | 4 | tasks, clinical_restriction_types, encounter_visit_types, document_types, nature_of_injury_types, observation_codes, storage_types, relation_types, users, locations |\n\nOf these 99 objects, 81 have descriptions; 18 (mostly escript_* and incident_* tables) have names only.\n\n### Export Interface\n\nThe export is initiated from **Control Panel → Interfaces → EHI Export** within WebChart:\n- **Administrators/SuperUsers** can export: a single patient, all patients for a provider, or all patients in the system\n- **Clinical users** can export: a single patient only\n- System requirements: 8GB RAM, ~100MB disk per patient exported\n- MIE offers assistance for customers lacking hardware capacity\n\n### Sample Export\n\nThe documentation includes a complete single-patient sample export (482KB JSON) for a test patient \"William S. Hart\" (pat_id 18, DOB 1954-11-30). The sample demonstrates:\n- Full demographic fields including SSN, insurance, emergency contacts\n- 2 appointments, 9 documents, 2 encounter orders, 1 encounter\n- 1 incident (workplace injury), 2 insurance policies\n- 3 patient conditions, 2 family conditions, 1 procedure\n- 2 medications, 2 allergies, 1 accommodation, 1 clinical restriction\n- Documents with Base64-encoded content\n- User references resolved inline with full user records\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nThis is one of the more thorough (b)(10) implementations encountered. The export explicitly addresses the **designated record set** by name and maps to internal database tables rather than to a standardized clinical model. This means it naturally captures data that a FHIR-only approach would miss.\n\n**Clearly covered domains:**\n- Demographics and identifiers (patients, patient_mrns, patient_extended_values)\n- Clinical encounters with full encounter data (encounters + extended values + orders + documents)\n- Medications and prescriptions, including full e-prescribing history (rxlist, escript_* tables)\n- Allergies (rxlist_allergylist)\n- Conditions, diagnoses, and problems (patient_conditions, patient_conditions_family)\n- Procedures (patient_procedures)\n- Lab results and observations (observations, lab_requests, observation_codes)\n- Orders (order_requests, order_items)\n- Documents with binary content in Base64 (documents, documents_txt, document_attachments)\n- DICOM imaging data (dicom_studies, dicom_series, dicom_images)\n- Insurance policies (insurance_policy, insurance_plans, insurance_pre_cert)\n- Appointments (appointments, apt_types)\n- Injections/immunizations (injections)\n- Dictation and transcription (dictation, dictation_documents, encounter_dictation)\n- Patient-provider messaging (conversation_messages)\n- Tasks and care coordination (tasks, tasklist_extended_values)\n- E-signatures (document_sign)\n- Patient relationships (pat_pat_relations, user_patients)\n\n**Strong occupational health coverage:**\n- Workplace incidents with full detail (incidents, incident_bls, incident_nibp, incident_links, incident_condition_link, incident_extended_fields)\n- Work accommodations and restrictions (accommodations, patient_clinical_restriction, accommodation_types, clinical_restriction_types)\n- Health surveillance panels (panel, panel_membership, panel_decert, patient_panel_status)\n- Audiometric data (audio, audio_baseline)\n- Pulmonary function tests (pft, pft_maneuver)\n- Respirator fit data (patient_respiratordetails)\n\n**Potentially missing or unclear:**\n- **Billing/claims detail**: While `insurance_policy` and `insurance_pre_cert` cover insurance and prior authorizations, there are no tables for charges, claims, payments, EOBs, or fee schedules. Given that WebChart integrates with external billing systems (OpenPM), detailed billing records may live outside the EHR. This is likely a genuine boundary of what WebChart stores rather than an export omission.\n- **Vitals as a distinct entity**: Vital signs appear to be stored in the generic `observations` table rather than as a separate entity, which is appropriate.\n- **Care plans and goals**: Not present as named entities, though they may be captured as encounter data or documents.\n- **Referrals**: No explicit referral table, though referral information may be captured in orders or documents.\n- **Patient portal data beyond messaging**: Portal questionnaire responses, self-check-in data — not clearly represented. May be captured as documents or observations.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\n- **Format**: Custom JSON mapping directly to MySQL database tables\n- **Standard**: Proprietary — not FHIR, C-CDA, or any external standard\n- **Appropriateness**: Highly appropriate for the data. A database-native export captures the full breadth of the product's data model, including occupational health data, incident details, and extended values that would be impossible to represent in FHIR US Core. This is exactly what (b)(10) should look like.\n- **Relationships**: Foreign keys are expressed through the nested array naming convention (`table.foreign_key`) and through inline resolution of referenced records (e.g., user records are embedded at every user_id reference). This is verbose but self-contained.\n- **Binary data**: Documents and images are Base64-encoded inline in the JSON. For large patient records with many documents, this could produce very large files (the docs suggest ~100MB per patient).\n- **Reconstructability**: A third party could reconstruct the patient record with reasonable effort. The JSON structure is self-documenting through field names, and the data dictionary provides table-level descriptions. However, **field-level descriptions are absent** — you get field names and sample values but must infer meaning from names alone.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n**Strengths:**\n- Clear, well-structured documentation on a modern documentation site\n- Explicit reference to 45 CFR 164.501 and §170.315(b)(10) — the vendor understands the regulatory requirement\n- Complete sample export with realistic data for a test patient\n- 99 database objects enumerated with descriptions\n- Export instructions with screenshots for both admin and clinical users\n- System requirements documented\n- Multi-patient export behavior documented (ZIP of individual JSON files)\n\n**Weaknesses:**\n- **No field-level documentation**: The data dictionary lists 99 objects with one-line descriptions, but there is no documentation of individual fields within those objects. A developer must infer field meanings from names and sample values.\n- **18 objects have no descriptions at all** — just names (e.g., escript_cancelrx, incident_bls, observation_flags)\n- **No data types specified**: All values in the JSON are strings (even numeric values like IDs), so there's no schema for intended types\n- **No value sets documented**: Coded fields (status codes, type codes) are not documented with their possible values\n- **No relationship/ERD documentation**: The foreign key relationships between tables are implied by the naming convention but not formally documented\n- **Single sample only**: Only one test patient is shown; no examples of complex multi-encounter, multi-document patients\n- **Date format**: Dates use MySQL's `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS` format; sentinel values like `0000-00-00 00:00:00` appear without explanation\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n- **Granularity**: Table-level names and descriptions ✓; Field-level names ✓ (in sample); Field-level descriptions ✗; Data types ✗; Value sets ✗; Cardinality ✗\n- **Coded field documentation**: Not present. Fields like `status`, `confirmed`, `canceled` use numeric codes without documented meanings.\n- **Relationship documentation**: Implicit through naming convention only. No entity-relationship diagram.\n- **Versioning**: The markdown frontmatter shows `date: 2023-11-16`, consistent with the published date. No change history.\n\nOverall, the data dictionary is **table-level only**, not field-level. The sample export fills the gap somewhat by showing actual field names and values, but a developer implementing an import would need significant reverse-engineering effort for coded fields, status values, and date conventions.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL (after redirects): https://docs.webchartnow.com/resources/system-specifications/electronic-health-information-ehi-export/ehi-export-technical-details/\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no (static HTML, no JavaScript required for content)\n- Navigation complexity: direct_link (registered URL contains all technical documentation)\n- Anti-bot issues: none (Cloudflare CDN but no challenge)\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n\n- **Google Drive source documents** (linked via \"Edit in Google Drive\" buttons on both pages) require Google authentication and are not publicly accessible.
